red contact with the company's stations. Ordinary care continued through equipment the ward understood. Supplies were checked, messages passed in person, and nobody mistook panic for a treatment plan.","Mara helped carry a chair, blankets, and Elena's little pot of mint. Her mother objected to the new window because the light made drawing difficult.","\"A political emergency should not be allowed to ruin every aspect of my day,\" she said.","Mara set the mint where it could catch the lamp. \"I'll submit a complaint.\"","\"In very large handwriting.\"","The ordinary irritation nearly undid her. She bent to kiss Elena's cheek and stayed there an extra moment. Her mother patted her arm, then asked for the pencil.","In the workshop, the others had arranged the maps and records into practical groups. Rowan could trace contracts and obtain addresses through contacts who did not depend on the altered directory. June could keep a record moving through more than one route. Ivo and Tessa could identify the stations and their supply links. Ada could make sure an unfamiliar volunteer understood which name belonged to which door. Noor would coordinate the people who could safely approach patients.","\"And us?\" Cassian asked.","Mara looked at the photograph of the court's evidence room on June's phone. Counsel had just sent a warning. The emergency committee had ordered the certified copies transferred to its in-house review unit by noon. The order was being challenged. The challenge might not be heard before the transport arrived.","Elena's statement. The hospital transfers. Cassian's examination report. The documents had survived private storage, fire, weather, and a collection of convenient errors. Now they were in a room whose protection might become the reason nobody else could reach them.","Mara remembered the door beside the hearing corridor: new brass around the latch, old wood under a badly finished repair. She had noticed it while trying not to watch the clerk carry away her flower.","\"We get the court record out before they can make it disappear inside a review,\" she said.","The room went quiet.","Cassian looked at the plan. \"The originals already have documented histories. We preserve that history when we move anything. No loose pages becoming a story only we can tell.\"","\"Agreed.\"","June would keep counsel informed through the channel that remained available. Rowan would find independent recipients for certified copies. They were not going to gather the only surviving record into one bag and call it safe. Some copies had to leave their hands.","Tamsin Holt called from the academy. June had asked whether the conservation archive could hold an independently documented copy of the public material. Holt said yes, provided nobody expected one damp basement to become the world's only safe place.","\"We have learned about distribution,\" Mara told her.","\"A relief. Lucien has offered his equipment notes. He says he refuses to lose access to good controls because a committee finds curiosity inconvenient.\"","Mara smiled. The academy had once been a door she needed to get through. Now people inside it were holding another door open, not because she had acquired an impressive name, but because they had watched her work.","They agreed on two separate recipients and an ordinary delivery route. No single person would carry everything. Holt promised to answer the telephone herself at dawn, which Mara suspected was the strongest part of the arrangement.","A message appeared on Cassian's phone. His former department had circulated a notice identifying him as a possible source of public strife and directing employees to refer contact with him to the emergency office. He read it, then set the phone face down.","Mara saw the restraint in the movement. \"They're frightened of your questions.\"","\"They may simply find my paperwork annoying.\"","\"A quality we share.\"","He smiled, but it did not reach his eyes. She crossed the room and took his hand in front of everybody. The gesture was not an answer to the notice. It was an answer to the loneliness she could feel trying to settle around him.","\"You're coming with me,\" she said.","\"I had hoped to retain my position as the man carrying the heavy things.\"","\"There may be brushes.\"","\"A grave professional development.\"","The laugh that moved around the table was small and necessary. It did not make the threat smaller. It made the people facing it more like themselves.","Just before three, they had the routes divided. Mercy House would remain a place of care, not a rallying point that invited a crowd into the wards. The old theater could carry messages through its staff and delivery network. Amina would protect Reed's private contact and keep his papers moving through the agreed process. Leda would not be asked to put her reunion on a public screen. The ordinary lives they had fought to restore would be part of the plan, not something to abandon until danger ended.","Mara found Cassian alone in the little kitchen, filling a flask. His collar was open, his coat hung over a chair, and the official pass was gone. He looked tired enough that she wanted to lock every door and keep him in the warm room with her.","Instead, she took the second cup from the shelf.","\"When this is over,\" she said, \"I want a morning in which you kiss me before telling me what is on the summons.\"","\"I believe I did.\"","\"Longer.\"","He put down the flask. \"A requirement I am prepared to fulfill immediately.\"","He drew her close. She met him halfway, her hands slipping under the open edges of his coat. The kiss was warm and unhurried for three stolen breaths. Then she rested her forehead against his and let herself say what the plans and jokes had been keeping steady.","\"I want the next mornings with you. All the ordinary, inconvenient ones.\"","His hand tightened at her back. \"Then we keep making them possible.\"","They did not promise that nobody would be hurt or that courage would always be rewarded. They took the flask, the papers, and the tools.","Outside, the city had become strangely quiet. A small white announcement van stood under the bridge, its speaker dark, a cheerful cartoon family painted across the door. The crew had gone into a café to wait for the dawn route.","Mara studied the van. Cassian followed her gaze.","\"That is an expression I recognize,\" he said.","She touched the silver flower inside her coat, then looked towards the courthouse three streets away.","\"Good,\" she said. \"I would hate to begin our next morning with a misunderstanding.\""],"questions":[{"type":"purpose","stem":"Why does the group arrange separate recipients and delivery routes for records?","options":["They want to remove every record from any future independent examination.","They want to preserve documented histories without placing all surviving material in one vulnerable They want to remove every record from any future independent examination.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. They want to preserve documented histories without placing all surviving material in one vulnerable collection.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. They want all copies to become anonymous stories without traceable histories.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. They believe a single private review unit should control all evidence.</button></div><div class=\"feedback\" hidden=\"hidden\"></div></div></section><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"70\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"72\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"v07c01","title":"1. The Van That Announced Its Own Theft","volume":"7","anchor":"chapter-0073","paragraphs":["The city announced that everything was under control at the exact moment Mara stole the announcement van. It was a small white vehicle with a giant speaker on the roof and a cartoon family painted across the door. Cassian climbed into the passenger seat, looked at the smiling father, and said, \"A remarkable likeness.\" Mara put the van into gear. \"You are the one with the perpetual expression of concern.\"","The speaker informed the empty street that the emergency administration would safeguard public security. All residents were to attend a memory-verification appointment. Any unauthorized gathering would be treated as a threat. Mara turned the volume down. She had no desire to help Selene dominate the neighborhood before breakfast. \"Is that legal?\" Cassian asked. \"The volume control? I have taken expert advice.\"","He laughed, then his hand closed around hers for a second before she needed it on the wheel. The courthouse lay three streets ahead. Its sealed evidence room held the certified copies of Elena's testimony, the hospital transfer records, and Cassian's examination report. Selene's emergency committee had ordered their transfer to an in-house review unit. By noon, the documents might become an omission in a very carefully edited narrative.","Cassian no longer possessed an investigator's authorization. He had surrendered the position after giving evidence about the damage to his own memory. The title remained, but a duke's name was poor protection against a government that had just declared him a source of strife. Mara had lost the false inheritance that once opened grand doors for her. Fortunately, neither of them had learned to rely exclusively on doors.","They parked beside an adjacent exhibition hall. A customary annual display about the city's founders filled its windows. Someone had covered an ivory frame with an advertisement for the upcoming emergency ceremony. The words COMMONWEALTH OF MEMORY made Mara's stomach tighten. \"An entity with ambitions,\" she said. Cassian studied the official logo. \"And a notable deficiency of shame.\"","The courthouse's rear entrance admitted cleaning staff at six. Mara wore a plain coat, carried a basket of brushes, and looked sufficiently annoyed to discourage conversation. Cassian had borrowed a cap from a traveling actor, who claimed it had once appeared in an avant-garde production of a cherry-tree-chopping legend. He looked too handsome for the role and not nearly cheerful enough for the play. Mara pushed a dirty cloth into his hands. \"Clean something. It will improve the description.\"","At the counter, a security assistant checked their bags. Mara asked whether the executive suite wanted its windows done before or after the archive. The man did not know. His intranet terminal kept asking him to refresh a page that would not load. A software update, the heading explained, was intended to streamline access. The result had been to shut out half the staff. He waved them through while he argued with the monitor.","They crossed a doorway and climbed the service stair. Cassian stopped behind a cluster of filing cabinets while Mara measured the old archive entrance. The brass around its latch had been replaced; the wooden frame had not. She ran her brush along a narrow split, found the repair she had noticed during the hearing, and smiled. A restoration shortcut had finally found a good cause.","\"Can you open it?\" Cassian whispered.","\"You consistently ask such hurtful questions.\"","\"I am trying to preserve your modesty.\"","\"Then stop looking at me like that.\"","She worked with the fine tool hidden among the brush handles. The latch gave after a careful adjustment. It was not mastery over every security procedure; it was familiarity with one badly repaired door. That was enough. Inside, the air smelled of paper and the hot machinery of an overworked copier. A librarian stood beside the evidence cabinet, clutching an album against her chest.","\"You are late,\" she said.","Mara blinked. \"We were expected?\"","\"June said somebody would come. She did not emphasize the basket.\"","The librarian's name was Tessa. She had watched the records migrate from one department to another over the years, acquiring new labels whenever an influential person needed an inconvenient document to disappear. Today she had been ordered to compile the destruction list. She had also been told that the certified material did not exist. The contradictory instructions had finally accomplished what twenty years of bad management had failed to do: they had made her rebel.","The album contained photographs of every page. The originals were still in the cabinet. Mara checked the case numbers against her handwritten list. Tessa had copied them accurately, including the statements that complicated Cassian's defence. Their usefulness depended on remaining a record rather than becoming a prettier story. Mara divided the copies into three packets, each small enough to hide in ordinary luggage. The originals would go to a separate safe location.","A heavy step sounded outside. Cassian moved to the door. Through the narrow glass, he saw a patrol approaching with a technician and a portable verification apparatus. The system required physical contact with its terminal; a radio announcement could not erase a memory. The new danger was more practical. The patrol could force people to use the apparatus and transfer the recent interval of their lives into a blank space they could not explain.","\"Two minutes,\" he said.","Tessa went pale. \"They will make me sit at it.\"","\"Not here,\" Mara replied. She took the librarian's hand and gave it a firm squeeze. \"Can you be part of a very poor cleaning performance?\"","Tessa looked from the basket to the door. \"I have worked here for twenty years. I can do poor performance anytime.\"","Mara opened the cupboard and found the archive's dust sheets. One became a cover for the packets. Another became a curtain across the aisle. Cassian wheeled the unwieldy copier toward the entrance. When the patrol reached the door, the three of them were confronting a monumental spill of paper and a machine that had apparently lost control of its own purpose. Tessa denounced it with the bitterness of an experienced employee.","\"The foreign contractor sold us this thing in July,\" she snapped. \"It was supposed to revolutionize records management. Now it eats originals. If you touch that lever, you will cancel the whole job.\"","The patrol leader looked at the jammed tray, the scattered sheets, and the gloomy librarian. \"We have orders.\"","\"So have I. Conflicting ones. Would you like to discuss them with the deputy?\"","Cassian kept his face down while he lifted a stack. Mara had to suppress a laugh. Tessa's habitual mildness had become a surprisingly savage weapon. The leader called the technician forward to examine the copier. Mara moved the covered trolley toward the service exit. The man reached for its cloth. She tipped the bucket beside it. Water spread across his boots.","\"Careful,\" she cried. \"The power lead!\"","The technician jumped back. Cassian pulled the copier clear of the puddle. In the resulting movement, Mara took control of the trolley and pushed it through the doorway. Tessa followed with the basket. They had reached the stair when an enraged voice behind them demanded the identity of the cleaners. The security assistant, still wrestling with the updated software, could supply no answer.","They ran. The trolley hit the landing rail and almost collided with the wall. Cassian caught its side, corrected the angle, and sent it down the ramp toward Mara. The patrol burst through the upper door. A command echoed down the stairwell. Mara took the first packet from beneath the cloth and threw it through an open delivery hatch. A pair of hands caught it. June's face appeared behind the glass. \"At last. I was beginning to feel underdressed for the occasion.\"","Outside, a locomotive whistle drowned the next shout. An old freight line crossed the street between the courthouse and the exhibition hall. The morning engine was moving slowly, pulling flat wagons toward the depot. June ducked behind it. Mara and Tessa followed. Cassian held the last wagon's rail, swung around its end, and landed beside them as the patrol reached the opposite curb. The moving train built a temporary wall between them.","The announcement van waited beyond the hall. Ivo sat at the wheel, looking miserably at the speaker. \"It will not stop telling me to report unauthorized behaviour.\" \"You may denounce us after the next turning,\" Mara said. Cassian lifted the evidence box into the back. They drove away under a cheerful recording urging citizens to collaborate with the emergency authorities. June gave the speaker a look of professional contempt.","They stopped behind a cooperative bakery where the ovens had been working since nightfall. A shipper from the port took one packet; an archaeologist from the academy took another. The third went to a newspaper printer whose press could operate without the city's web. Cyberspace had become too easily monitored. Paper could still cross a street in a basket of bread. It was slower than an intranet, less adaptable, and remarkably difficult to suppress everywhere at once.","Mara called Mercy House from a borrowed line. Elena was awake. Noor had moved her room away from the new street terminal; a separate supply arrangement protected the patients from undertreatment during the emergency. Her mother sounded tired, but her voice was unchanged. \"Have you eaten?\" she asked. Mara closed her eyes for one warm second. \"I am in a bakery.\" \"That is not an answer.\" Cassian put a plum bun in her free hand.","After the call, June spread a street map across the counter. The emergency patrols were a real force, but Selene could not govern every alley, family, workshop, and kitchen as a single controllable machine. Their defence would have to be a web as well: people able to communicate, preserve records, and help neighbors move. Mara took a pencil. She had no army and no executive suite. She did have partners who had come back for her. \"Shall we organize a little inconvenience?\" she asked. Cassian kissed the flour from her cheek. \"For my part, I consider it a vocation.\""],"questions":[{"type":"Evidence / selection","stem":"Why does Mara retain the statements that complicate Cassian's defense?","options":["The value of the copies depends on preserving the record rather than selecting only convenient material.","She believes a copied statement is automatically more accurate than an original.","The statements establish that every accusation against him is correct.","The emergency committee has required a more favorable narrative."],"answer":0,"explanation":"她保留不利或复杂部分，因为副本应忠实于记录，而非只讲更好听的故事。文本没有说这些内容证明所有指控成立，更未说复制会自动提升准确性。","evidence":[18],"shuffled":true}],"wordCount":1676,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0073\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">7</div><h1>1. She believes a copied statement is automatically more accurate than an original.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. The Best Use of a Department Store","volume":"7","anchor":"chapter-0074","paragraphs":["Captain Rook arrived at the bakery with a toolbox, a pot of boiled eggs, and a message from the harbor. The workers had agreed to cooperate, provided nobody called them a militia. \"Some of us are accustomed to being paid,\" she said. \"The distinction matters.\" Rowan took an egg. \"A reasonable concern. I intend to remain a deeply inconvenient shareholder.\"","Leah followed her inside carrying a rolled chart. The port strike had taught her how to organize communication when an employer tried to monopolize every official channel. A walking messenger, a notice hidden in an ordinary delivery list, and a dependable meeting time could do useful work without a web-based platform. \"No genius required,\" she said. \"Only people who will actually arrive.\" Ivo looked at the clock. \"You are already more demanding than most management.\"","The immediate task was to get supplies to Mercy House. A patrol had established a verification point at the bridge, where the road narrowed around a traffic island. Ordinary medicine deliveries were being delayed while their drivers attended interviews. The restriction posed a threat to patients even before anyone touched the memory apparatus. A sense of security was difficult to preserve when a stranger could detain your pharmacist for two hours and call the delay protection.","Noor had sent a clear listing of what the ward needed. The list was not a shopping opportunity for amateurs. Rook would carry the sealed medical packages; the grocer would send food in a second vehicle. Mara would scout the bridge, and Cassian would keep the return route open. Leah was point person for the workers at the other end. It was a partnership assembled from very different skills, and the first test was whether they could resist interfering with one another's jobs.","Mara pulled a department store uniform over her clothes and added a gray cap. The embroidered name on the jacket belonged to an amiable salesman named Colin, who had recently offered the use of the store's loading bay. \"I adore the fit,\" Rowan said. \"You look like a woman about to refuse my refund.\" \"I have studied your credit-card account,\" she replied. \"There will be no mercy.\"","Cassian straightened her collar. \"You have a label showing.\"","\"A fatal flaw in an otherwise magnificent disguise.\"","\"I am told excellence requires attention to details.\"","She caught his hand before he could step away and kissed his knuckles. \"Try to come back with the same number of these.\"","He looked at her, warmth briefly displacing the concern. \"That is a very modest request.\"","The bridge patrol had brought its own publicity team. A correspondent interviewed a nervous official beside a portrait of Selene. The new establishment described the devices as a way to restore normality. A poster referred to recent evidence as \"the fabrication of a disgraced heiress and her militant protectors.\" June took a photograph. \"An excellent use of my name,\" she said. \"They have misspelled it.\"","A second pamphlet attempted to blacken the patients' testimony by citing unverified claims about cocaine use and violent behavior. Noor had already challenged the allegations. Mara wanted to tear the pamphlet down. June stopped her with one touch. \"Later. We need a copy, not a confrontation.\" She folded it into her bag. Her expression promised that somebody would regret having printed an allegation they could not support.","At the curb, an immigrant delivery driver stood beside his van, arguing with an attendant. His authorization was valid, but the patrol had told him that a new category of clearance applied. The packages in the van required timely delivery. The driver pointed to the labels, then to the clock. A broad-shouldered guard called him a nuisance. His English grew more careful as the guard's tone became more offensive. Mara stepped into the space before the next shove.","\"Colin's department store,\" she announced, holding out the borrowed badge. \"Which queue is for commercial deliveries?\"","The guard gestured vaguely toward the verification line.","\"That line has no loading platform. If I leave six crates there, your staff will be constantly moving them. My manager will ask who approved the delay.\"","\"I approved it.\"","\"Wonderful. Could you write that down?\"","The request had less aggressiveness than his challenge and considerably more practical menace. She held out a pen. The guard examined the badge. Behind him, a supervisor was trying to restrain an argument between two of his own attendants. Nobody had a consistent answer to the same question. Mara made herself one more complication in the least dramatic way possible: cheerful, conversational, and unwilling to disappear.","While she held his attention, the delivery driver saw Leah on the far side of the bridge. She made a small signal toward the bypass road. He did not move yet. A collision in the narrow gap would harm everyone and accomplish nothing. He waited until a municipal garbage truck reached the barrier. Its driver had no intention of carrying rotting food to a medical interview. The ensuing argument drew the supervisor away.","Then the van turned. The maneuver was swift enough to be ordinary. It followed the permitted service lane around the traffic island and joined a line of store deliveries beyond the bridge. Rook's vehicle was already in that line. The port workers shifted a concrete barrier back to its usual position after both had passed. Nothing had been broken. A piece of organized obstruction had briefly encountered a better-organized understanding of the road.","The guard finally handed Mara a slip with his name on it. \"There. Now take your crates elsewhere.\"","\"With pleasure.\"","She accepted the pen, folded the paper, and walked toward the department store. June strolled beside her. \"I should nominate you for a Pulitzer Prize.\" \"For moving a delivery van?\" \"For keeping a straight face while inventing a six-crate tragedy.\" \"There were eggs. In comparative terms, it was a vulnerable shipment.\"","They were almost at the loading bay when the publicity camera turned toward them. Someone in the interview team recognized Mara. A shout went up. She caught June's sleeve and ran through the store's open doors. A window display full of pearly lamps flashed past. They took the aisle between kitchen tubs and bedding, disturbing an elderly customer who informed the pursuing guard that running in shops was disgraceful.","Mara and June reached the rear stair. The guard gained on them at the turn. June thrust a bunch of long-handled dusters between the rail and his path. He stopped rather than drive his face into the handles. Mara pushed open the delivery exit. Outside waited Cassian, leaning against a modestly battered car as though he had invited them to lunch. \"Did the shopping go well?\" \"We found several items of interest,\" Mara said, sliding inside.","He drove before the guard reached the door. An attempt to follow them was defeated by the delivery driver, who had returned in the now-empty van and chosen that moment to unload a crate. The guard had to stop short of the obstruction. In the rear window, Mara saw the driver's delighted grin. She would remember it much longer than the official's threat.","At Mercy House, the eggs had arrived intact. So had the medicine. Agnes was sorting the supplies with the ward staff, and Noor was speaking to an anxious family. A visiting neuroscientist had joined her to analyze the available data on the devices. There was no known universal remedy, no injection that made a person immune, and no reason to pretend otherwise. There were concrete ways to reduce unnecessary exposure, protect records, and keep ordinary treatment working. The team would use them.","Mara found Elena in the workshop room, sitting beside a small pot of transplanted mint. Her mother had good hours and tired hours. This was a good one. She had been helping Pip arrange the alphabet on a homemade sign. The sign currently read NO VERY BAD MEN, with a question mark beneath it. \"We are still working on our admission policy,\" Elena said. Mara bent to hug her, holding the warmth for one second longer than usual.","\"I missed a label on your coat,\" Elena murmured.","\"So did my adviser.\"","Cassian, at the door, accepted the criticism without defense. \"I shall submit to retraining.\"","The warmth did not last long enough to become an illusion that the danger had ended. A new notice demanded that the hospital surrender its patient listing by evening. The corporate board described the demand as an aid to coordination. Noor refused to send clinical names through a channel she could not scrutinize. An official decision would be needed, but the hospital's families could not wait passively for one.","Leah proposed using the workers' existing routes to carry sealed messages to the families. The collaboration would not ask residents to join a strike or adopt a position on unionism. It would give them the information needed to make their own choices. Several volunteered a spare room or a vehicle. The librarian offered to sift duplicate messages so a missed delivery would be noticed. Small pieces of dependable work began to form a framework that could bear more than any single hero.","That evening, a retired mailman arrived with letters from three neighborhoods. A software engineering instructor offered space in a classroom. An Asian-American women's association had sent food through a local member and promised funds for independent legal aid. A lawyer, recently put on notice by the municipality, brought the emergency order and several pronounced objections. None could retake the city alone. Together they could make it harder to isolate one street at a time.","Cassian watched Mara put the messages into piles. She had become the person others looked toward when the room grew uncertain. He stepped behind her and set a steady hand at her waist. \"You are allowed to be tired.\" \"I know.\" \"You are also allowed to eat the eggs.\" She looked down at the plate beside the map. \"I thought that was a strategic resource.\" \"It is. I am defending it from Rowan.\" From the table, Rowan raised his fork. \"The aggressiveness of this regime is intolerable.\""],"questions":[{"type":"Motive / action","stem":"What is the immediate purpose of Mara's request that the guard put his approval in writing?","options":["To obtain authorization to close every verification station.","To prove that the company's entire emergency order has been cancelled.","To occupy him with an accountable delivery decision while the supplies can take another route.","To persuade the patients to accept delayed medicine delivery."],"answer":2,"explanation":"她让守卫面对自己批准延误的书面责任，借熟悉的工作麻烦牵住其注意；同时运药司机等待可行旁道。作用有具体范围，不能扩大成取消全部命令。","evidence":[18,19,20],"shuffled":true}],"wordCount":1684,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0074\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">7</div><h1>2.
